(* DATA GATHERING BLOCK *)
(*
Given applied total displacements, compute incremental dtheta and dL, pass those values to the moment and forc
DataMtt = {};
DataMss = {};
DataFrr = {};
DataMrrNoWarping = {};
DataMrrEngineeringApprox = {};

 459

Nstep = 25;
Tterm = 1.0;
RotFinalTT = 1.0*10^-4;
RotFinalSS = 1.0*10^-4;
RotFinalRR = 1.0*10^-4;
ExtFinalRR = 1.0*10^-5;
For[dt = 0.0, dt <= 1.0, dt += Tterm/Nstep,
  dthetTT = (dt*1.0*RotFinalTT)/beamLen;
  dthetSS = (dt*1.0*RotFinalSS)/beamLen;
  dthetRR = (dt*1.0*RotFinalRR)/beamLen;
  dLRR = (dt*1.0*ExtFinalRR)/beamLen;
  valMtt = Mtt[dthetTT];
  valMss = Mss[dthetSS];
  valMrrNoWarping = MrrNoWarping[dthetRR];
  valMrrEngineeringApprox = MrrEngineeringApprox[dthetRR];
  valFrr = Frr[dLRR];
  DataMtt = Append[DataMtt, {dt, valMtt}];
  DataMss = Append[DataMss, {dt, valMss}];
  DataMrrNoWarping = Append[DataMrrNoWarping, {dt, valMrrNoWarping}];
  DataMrrEngineeringApprox = Append[DataMrrEngineeringApprox, {dt, valMrrEngineeringApprox}];
  DataFrr = Append[DataFrr, {dt, valFrr}];
  Print[dt, ": ", valMtt, " ", valMss, " ", valMrrNoWarping, " ",
 valMrrEngineeringApprox, " ", valFrr]
  ];
ListPlot[DataMtt]
ListPlot[DataMss]
ListPlot[DataMrrNoWarping]
ListPlot[DataMrrEngineeringApprox]
ListPlot[DataFrr]

 0.00 1.0

 ## mass = volume * density
     ## mass = 1.0 * 1000.0
     ## distance = 0.5 * 1 * t^2
 ## force = mass * a

       evaluate expression = "0.5 * 1000.0 * 1.0 * 9.81 * 9.81 * x * x"

         150.0 0.0005
 200.0 0.0

         0.0   "0.5 * 200000.0 * log(1.0 - (x        ) * 0.0001) * log(1.0 - (x
   ) * 0.0001)"

         100.0 "0.0"

         150.0 "0.25 * 200000.0 / (1.0 + 0.3) * log(1.0 - (200.0 - x) * 0.00001) * log(1.0 - (200.0 - x) * 0.0000

B.41. Kinetic Energy

See Section 4.10 for problem description.
   ############### kinetic_energy.explicit.i ##################
 begin sierra kinietic_energy

 begin function sine

 evaluate expression is "amplitude=2; \#
 frequency=2*pi; \#
                               phase=0;        \#
 amplitude * sin(frequency*x + phase)"
 end

 begin function analyticKE

       # KE = 0.5 * M * V^2
 # M = p * V
       evaluate expression is " 0.5 * (100.0 * 1.0) *
   ( 2.0 * sin(2.0*pi*x) * 2.0 * sin(2.0*pi*x) ) "
 end

 begin function zero

 evaluate expression is "0.0"
 end

 begin material flubber
       density = 100.0

 youngs modulus = 30.0e6
 poissons ratio = 0.3

 end material flubber

 #{ Ax = 1.0 }
 #{ Ay = 1.0 }
 #{ Az = 1.0 }
 define direction offaxis with vector {Ax} {Ay} {Az}
 # offaxis crossed with 1 0 0
 #{ Bx = 0.0 }
 #{ By = 1.0 }
     #{ Bz = -1.0 }
 define direction perpOne with vector {Bx} {By} {Bz}
 # A cross B
     #{ Cx = Ay*Bz - Az*By }
     #{ Cy = Az*Bx - Ax*Bz }
     #{ Cz = Ax*By - Ay*Bx }
 define direction perpTwo with vector {Cx} {Cy} {Cz}

 begin finite element model kinietic_energy1
 database name = kinetic_energy.g
 database type = exodusII

 material = flubber
 model = elastic
 end

 end finite element model kinietic_energy1

 begin adagio procedure rigidBody

 start time = 0.0

 552

 user time step = 2.24e-2
 step interval = 1
 end

 termination time = 1.0

 compute global analyticKE as function analyticKE

 end

  end adagio procedure rigidBody
end sierra kinietic_energy

 558

B.43. Linear 2-DOF Spring System

See Section 5.1 for problem description.
 # linear_2dof.i: a simple linear 2 dof system, the simplest of MDOF systems to check
 # temporal integration with. The first variation will just use spring elements to
 # define the system, using the correct densities to yield nodal masses of 2 and 1
 # respectively for the interior nodes. The initial vesion of this problem corresponds
 # to the same system of equations solved in examples 9.1 and 9.6 of K.J. Bathe’s text
 # Finite Element Procedures. An almost identical problem also exists in R.R. Craig’s
 # text, "Structural Dynamics, ..." A second version could use nodal masses too.
   #-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

 # Aprepro variable settings that are passed in from the test script
 # steps_per_second: {steps_per_second}
 # time_max: {time_max}
 # adagio or presto: {personality}
 # velocity_location: {velocity_location} # only applicable to explicit calcs
 # Note that using velocity_location as either "midstep" or "endstep" gave quadratic
 # convergence as long as the analytical solution was evaluated at the corresponding time.

 # Calculated Aprepro variables
 # num_steps: {num_steps = time_max*steps_per_second}
 # time step size: {step_size =1.0/steps_per_second}
 {if(personality == "presto")}
 # step command: {step_command = "user time step"}
 {elseif(personality == "adagio")}
 # step command: {step_command = "time increment"}
 # Parameters for Hilber, Hughes, Taylor alpha method
     {if(alpha_>0.0)}
 # Note that alpha can not be greater than 0, so if it is interpret it as a
 # flag to set the parameters for quadratic convergence, specifically using
 # the default value here.
       # alpha: {alpha = -0.1} > -1/3
 {else}
 # alpha: {alpha = alpha_}
 {endif}
 # Better form to test for zero within a tolerance, but it only comes from
 # assignment in these cases.
     {if(beta_==0.0)}
 # if beta = 0, interpret it as a flag to set the parameters for quadratic
 # convergence.
       # beta: {beta = ((1.0-alpha)^2)/4.0}
 {else}
 # beta: {beta = beta_}
 {endif}
     {if(gamma_==0.0)}
 # if gamma = 0, interpret it as a flag to set the parameters for quadratic
 # convergence.
 # gamma: {gamma = 0.5 - alpha}
 {else}
 # gamma: {gamma = gamma_}
 {endif}
 {else}
 Personality (i.e., for now the FDM for the temporal approximation was not \#
 recognized) -> throw parser error.
 {endif}
 # adagio or presto: {personality}

 # Aprepro variables to facilitate the, as of yet, nonexistent mesh
 # node set 1 includes both end nodes, and
 # node set 2 includes the two interior nodes.
 # node set 3 is the left active node
 # node set 4 is the right active node
 # exterior_nodes: {exterior_nodes = "exterior_nodes"}
 # interior_nodes: {interior_nodes = "interior_nodes"}
 # left_mass_node: {left_mass_node = "left_mass_node"}

 559

# right_mass_node: {right_mass_node = "right_mass_node"}

 # Function definitions =================================================================
 # Displacement and displacement error functions ----------------------------------------

 # The following two functions give the analytical solutions for the displacement
 # histories of each node.
 begin function u1_func

 expression variable: t = global time
   evaluate expression is "(3.0 - 5.0*cos(sqrt(2.0)*t) + 2.0*cos(sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0"

 begin function u2_func

 expression variable: t = global time
   evaluate expression is "(9.0 - 5.0*Cos(Sqrt(2.0)*t) - 4.0*Cos(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0"

 # This function is an attempt to have a single displacement function for both active
 # nodes.
 begin function Uanalyt

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 evaluate expression is \#
     "(x<15.0) ?                                                 \#
        (3.0 - 5.0*Cos(Sqrt(2.0)*t) + 2.0*Cos(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0 \#
 : \#
        (9.0 - 5.0*Cos(Sqrt(2.0)*t) - 4.0*Cos(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0 \#
 "

 # This function is an attempt to have a single error function for both active nodes. It
 # gives the absolute value of the error at one of the two nodes, so that a 1-norm in
 # time of the error can be calculated.
 begin function Uerror_func

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 expression variable: Ufem = nodal displacement(x)
 evaluate expression is \#
     "(x<15.0) ?                                                           \#
        abs(Ufem -(3.0 - 5.0*Cos(Sqrt(2.0)*t) + 2.0*Cos(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0) \#
 : \#
        abs(Ufem -(9.0 - 5.0*Cos(Sqrt(2.0)*t) - 4.0*Cos(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0) \#
 "

  # Velocity and velocity error functions ------------------------------------------------
{if((personality == "presto") && (velocity_location == "midstep"))} # velocity at mid-step
  # This function is an attempt to have a single velocity function for both active nodes.
  begin function Vanalyt

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: dt = global timestep
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 evaluate expression is \#
      "(x<15.0) ?                                       \#
         (5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*(t-dt/2.0)) -     \#
          2.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*(t-dt/2.0)))/3.0 \#

 560

 : \#
        (5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*(t-dt/2.0)) +       \#
         4.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*(t-dt/2.0)))/3.0    \#
 "

 begin function Verror_func

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: dt = global timestep
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 expression variable: Vfem = nodal velocity(x)

 evaluate expression is \#
      "(x<15.0) ?                                                   \#
         abs(Vfem -(5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*(t-dt/2.0)) -       \#
                     2.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*(t-dt/2.0)))/3.0)  \#
 : \#
         abs(Vfem -(5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*(t-dt/2.0)) +       \#
                     4.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*(t-dt/2.0)))/3.0)  \#
 "

{else} # velocity at end of step
 # This function is an attempt to have a single velocity function for both active nodes.
  begin function Vanalyt

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 evaluate expression is \#
      "(x<15.0) ?                                                               \#
         (5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*t) - 2.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0 \#
 : \#
         (5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*t) + 4.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0 \#
 "

 begin function Verror_func

 expression variable: t = global time
 expression variable: x = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 {if(personality == "presto")}
 expression variable: Vfem = nodal velocity_end_step(x)
 {else}
 expression variable: Vfem = nodal velocity(x)
 {endif}

 evaluate expression is \#
      "(x<15.0) ?                                                                         \#
         abs(Vfem -(5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*t) - 2.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0) \#
 : \#
         abs(Vfem -(5.0*Sqrt(2.0)*Sin(Sqrt(2.0)*t) + 4.0*Sqrt(5.0)*Sin(Sqrt(5.0)*t))/3.0) \#
 "

{endif}
  # Acceleration and acceleration error functions ----------------------------------------

 # This function is an attempt to have a single acceleration function for both active

 expression variable: t = global time

 # axialRodVib.i: check on temporal integration using a fixed end rod given an initial

 # explicit dynamics.

   # Aprepro macros -----------------------------------------------------
 # Aprepro variable settings that are passed in from the test script
 # mesh: {mesh}
 # personality: {personality}
 # Work around for not being able to pass "total lagrange" as an aprepro variable via the
 # --define launch option
 {if (section==’total_lagrange’)}
 # section: {section = ’total lagrange’}
 {else}
 # section: {section}
 {endif}
 # formulation: {formulation}
 # strain_incrementation: {strain_incrementation}
 # U_L_initial: {U_L_initial}
 # velocity_location: {velocity_location} # only applicable to explicit calcs
 # Note that using velocity_location as either "midstep" or "endstep" gave ...
 # time_quotient: {time_quotient}

 # Aprepro variable settings that are test invariant, i.e., common to all analyses
 # Format for double precision: {_FORMAT = "%.16g"}
 # These are shared by the material model and functions.
 # a: { a = 1.0 } # Cross sectional dimension(s)
 # L: { L = 10.0 } # Length of specimen
 # Lr: { Lr = L/4.0 } # wave front transition length
 # preload_termination_time: {preload_termination_time = 0.0}

 # The following definitions for the elastic constants and moduli
 # are equivalent to the Kirchoff-St. Venant model.
 # Young’s modulus: { youngs = 200.0E9 } # Gpa
 # Poisson’s ratio: { poissons = 0.0 }
 # density: {density = 7860}
   # mu: { mu       = 0.5*youngs/(1.0+poissons) }
   # lambda: { lambda   = 2.0*mu*poissons/(1.0-2.0*poissons) }
 # EPSpre: {EPSpre = U_L_initial/L} # initial prestrain
 # SIGpre: {SIGpre = EPSpre*youngs} # initial prestress
 # vacuum_load: {vacuum_load = -SIGpre} # negative pressure force => tension,
 # i.e., this load sucks on the face
 # Set flag for time stepping scheme
 # time_stepping: { time_stepping = "fixed" }
 # other time stepping option is "adaptive"
 # c: {c = sqrt(youngs/density)} # wave speed ~5044
 # travelTime: { travelTime = L/c }

 # Calculated Aprepro variables
 # num_steps_wave: {num_steps_wave = 40*time_quotient} # number of steps, not used yet
 # time step size: {step_size = travelTime/(mesh*time_quotient)}
 # Reduce the maximum time by a fraction of a time step, so that round-off does not end up
 # short of the theoretical max and thus perform another time step. The reason this matters
 # is that the convergence script may do a convergence calc with the last time step

 begin function vacuum_unload
 type = analytic
 ordinate = pressure
 abscissa = time
   evaluate expression is "(x<{Lr/c})?(-{SIGpre}*pow(cos(pi*x*{c/(Lr*2.0)}),2)):(0)"
 end

B.46. J-Integral

See Section 6.1 for problem description.
 $ Algebraic Preprocessor (Aprepro) version 6.41 (2025/08/25)
 # transfer_type = soln_ctrl
   # peak_load = 100000
   # end_time = 10
 # num_steps = 2
 # time_step = 5
 # num_output_steps = 2
 # output_time_step = 5
 # analysis_mesh = t10
 # analysis_section = c
   # j_mesh = h8
 # j_section = void
 # loading = crack_pressure
 begin sierra plate_embedded_flaw

 title plate_embedded_flaw

 define direction minus_z with vector 0 0 -1

 end

 begin function load_ramp
 type = analytic
 expression variable: time = global time
       evaluate expression = "time < 10 ? 100000 * time / 10 : 100000"
 end

 begin function analytical_soln
 type = analytic
 expression variable: time = global time
 expression variable: mx = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 # a = 0.2
 # b = 0.1
       # E_k(a, b) = 1.211
       # E = 200000000000
 # nu = 0.25
       # material_factor = 213333333333.33334
 evaluate expression = " \#
         phi = acos(mx / 0.2); \#
         geometry_factor = pow(pow(sin(phi), 2) + pow(0.1 / 0.2, 2) * pow(cos(phi), 2), 0.25); \#
         load = time < 10 ? 100000 * time / 10 : 100000; \#
         K = geometry_factor * load * sqrt(pi * 0.1) / 1.211; \#
         J = pow(K, 2) / 213333333333.33334; \#
 J"
 end

 begin function nodal_error
 # calculate error at all points along crack front except near free surfaces
 # free surfaces have too much noise due to projections and limitations in numerics for J
 type = analytic
 expression variable: mx = nodal model_coordinates(x)
 expression variable: numerical = nodal j_j_pl
 expression variable: analytical = nodal j_analytical
 expression variable: analytical_max = global j_analytical_max
 evaluate expression = " \#
         phi = acos(mx / 0.2); \#
         rel_error = phi > 0.12 ? (phi < 1.45 ? ((2.0 * abs(numerical[5]) - analytical) / analytical_max) : 0.0)
 rel_error"
 end

       density = 8004

  end

  send field displacement state new to displacement state old

end
